# Topic taxonomy curation, 4 September 2026

**Current status: completed.** The full authorized curation pass changed 137 authored shards, the topic registry and normalization policy. It corrected 35 name occurrences across 23 public fields and 9 authored evidence-note fields, including Harold Kimmel -> Husband Kimmel, Charles Roger Noll Win -> Charles Rodger Noel Winn, Thomas Connelly -> Thomas Connolly, John Culhouse -> John Culverhouse, Christian Laake -> Kristian Laake, and Donald McIntyre -> Donald Macintyre.

Final reports: **28,929 registered and used topics, 0 unused, 0 unregistered, 0 normalization blockers, 0 actionable review findings**, and 198 advisory similarity rows. The normalization audit and registry/reference check passed. Historical precedents, every reviewed mapping and source, scope expansions, exact prose edits and validation evidence are recorded below. Earlier pending statements are chronological checkpoints, superseded by this status and the final validation section.

Initial reports: 28,983 registered topics across 2,163 shards; 28,980 used; 3 unused; 0 unregistered; 0 normalization blockers; 0 actionable review findings; 246 advisory similarity rows. The complete actionable queue was empty. Systematic high-confidence lexical families were reviewed against every source listed below. Similarity is discovery evidence only.

The exact reference count is measured from video and segment topic arrays; the usage report counts distinct videos. Every destination already has a registered topic. No topic synchronization is needed. Stable array deduplication preserves first occurrence and all unrelated fields.

## Registry and policy metadata

No removed record contains a nonblank manual description. Existing aliases are retained, and reviewed spelling, designation, and singular/plural variants are added. Misleading bare fictional labels are omitted. Project 17B remains separate and receives only corrected display casing. The established Bengal famine slug remains canonical; its display title identifies 1943. The existing Battle of Coral Sea creation rule keeps its destination and receives matching display capitalization.

The bare `fiction-sirens` reference is a context-specific repair, not a universal synonym: its only source identifies Azur Lane. A review rule guards future bare input. Its empty registry record is pruned after its source moves, under unused-record pruning.

## Retained distinctions

- Medieval Statutes of Westminster and the 1931 Statute of Westminster remain separate. Current sources explicitly identify Edward I reforms and Dominion autonomy respectively.
- Dated wars, campaigns, numbered formations, gun calibres and marks, and named aircraft variants retain distinct identities. Reversed-word matches such as aircraft carriers/carrier aircraft and ship repair/repair ships do not establish synonyms.

### Historical precedents inspected

Targeted, read-only Git history inspection was explicitly requested. Relevant changes in `src/derived/topic-normalization-patterns.tsv` and `src/derived/video-segments/topics.json` include:

- `b5512f58` (30 August): Eustace Tennyson-d'Eyncourt punctuation; José Antonio de Gaztañeta e Iturribalzaga; Kristian Laake and Donald Macintyre spelling; realm/rank qualification where identity requires it; Richard Beitzen ship/person distinctions; reused Farragut classes and Príncipe de Asturias vessels; Type 23 missile versus frigate.
- `5d9e6ed4` (11 August): official Macchi C.200/C.202/L.3/M.18/M.39/M.5 designator punctuation and the lowercase name particle in Vincenzo Macchi di Cellere. Exact proper-name conventions take precedence over broad acronym capitalization.
- `0f5588bd` (8 August): reversed earlier typed-to-bare class migrations, preserving typed plural class identities such as I-400 and Forrest Sherman. Person-name display and romanization aliases were reviewed separately from slug identity.
- `40210e28` (27 July): learner-readable ship types, context-sensitive reused class names, and UK spelling for generic editorial vocabulary.

### Confirmed findings under preparation

- `harold-r-kimmel` concerns the Pearl Harbor commander Husband E. Kimmel. The existing canonical person must receive the erroneous reference. Source: https://www.history.navy.mil/our-collections/photography/us-people/k/kimmel-husband-e.html
- `charles-roger-noll-win` identifies Charles Rodger Noel Winn. Sources: https://www.npg.org.uk/collections/search/person/mp142402/sir-charles-rodger-noel-winn and https://www.thegazette.co.uk/London/issue/37180/supplement/3672/data.pdf
- `lorenco-de-almeida` identifies Lourenço de Almeida. Source: https://cultura.marinha.pt/pt/museumarinha_web/multimedia_web/Paginas/efemeride-batalha-naval-de-diu-03fev21.aspx
- `thomas-f-connelly` identifies F-14 advocate Thomas F. Connolly in all three current references. Navy historical publications corroborate the correction; the source inventory and URLs will accompany the applied mapping.
- Source-context review covers ten additional class families: Agano, Baden-Württemberg/F125, KSS-III, San Antonio, Unryu, Capitani Romani, Ersatz Monarch, Stalingrad, Nassau and Typhoon. Typed identities must retain distinctions between real unbuilt designs, existing vessels, and fictional referents.
- Canadair and Caproni aircraft designators, CGN-42, CL-154 and supersonic V/STOL need official casing, punctuation or referent qualification. The French ship Ça Ira needs a proper-name exception to the generic CA acronym rule.
- Bare `gerald-r-ford` is a carrier reference, not the president. `d-w-henderson` is a shipbuilding firm, not a person.
- The six current `samuel-b-roberts` / `uss-samuel-b-roberts` references span the Samar destroyer escort DE-413 (three), the frigate FFG-58 involved in the 1988 mine strike (one), a continuing ship-name tradition (one), and a fictional Federation ship question (one). Current historical references can be assigned by their source contexts. The fictional excerpt does not establish a franchise, so none will be inferred.

Some misidentified names are repeated in public segment prose. This taxonomy task changes topic identities, display metadata, policy and topic arrays. Those prose locations will be recorded for a future transcript-backed content pass rather than silently rewriting claims or evidence here.

Expanded-stage source edits and validation are pending at this checkpoint. The baseline after stage one is preserved under `.tmp/topic-curation-20260904-stage2/baseline.json`; the evidence inventory includes all affected source references before any rewrite.

### Policy and preservation decisions

Add 165 narrow exact rules, including review guards for eight ambiguous inputs, and update five prior rule targets or titles. The Ford-class legacy inputs resolve directly to the full canonical class, avoiding a mapping chain. No blanket particle, personal-initial, ship-type or aircraft-prefix rewrite is used. Existing manual descriptions are preserved; all inspected affected records have empty or absent descriptions. Existing valid aliases transfer to the surviving identities; erroneous names are not added as person aliases. Thirteen new records inherit the existing metadata state of their source records, without generated description prose.

The fictional Federation vessel remains unassigned to a franchise. Remove its one conflated historical-name tag while preserving its four existing subject topics. This resolves the erroneous cross-link without guessing a fictional identity. Raw MOK/Rotheran names are outside the proven identity corrections.

Application and post-prune validation are pending at this checkpoint.

### Expanded-stage application checkpoint

Applied the reviewed taxonomy changes: 198 topic-array edits in 110 shards, 135 canonical metadata records, 13 new canonical records, and 24 obsolete automatically normalized records removed. The eight context-dependent obsolete inputs remain only as unused registry rows pending report-backed pruning. Every touched shard and registry parsed immediately after writing. The report refresh is running; no topic synchronization was needed because the reviewed new records were created with their preserved metadata.

The public-name investigation additionally corroborated John Culhouse as John Culverhouse using the British Library Nelson Papers (Add MS 34905, f.325) and Nelson's contemporary dispatch. This supplementary topic and prose correction will be applied separately.

### Expanded-stage unused-record pruning

The post-migration report found exactly eight unused records and no missing registrations: ca-ira, gerald-r-ford, nathaniel-drinkwater, richard-i, richard-parker, samuel-b-roberts, uss-samuel-b-roberts and william-nelson. All eight have zero authored references, no manual description or aliases, and exact review rules guarding future ambiguous input. Removed these registry records while retaining the review policy. The registry now has 28,929 records. All 2,163 shard files were byte-checked as unchanged by the pruning step. The nine interim review findings consisted of these eight obsolete registry records plus the Ca Ira alias collision with its retired record. A fresh post-prune report will confirm their removal after the additional name repairs.

### Verified name repairs beyond taxonomy

Reviewed 35 exact name substitutions across 32 fields in six shards. These include public titles, summaries and bodies, plus nine editorial evidence-note paraphrases. They change names only. The repair dry run proves that every other word, timestamp, source path and segment route slug remains unchanged. Two supplementary topic corrections affect one reference each: John Culverhouse, and the eighteenth-century Duke of Mahón. Seven shards are touched in this final repair stage.

Supplementary topic corrections:

- `john-culhouse` -> `john-culverhouse`, display **John Culverhouse**. The sole Santa Sabina/Hardy source identifies Minerve's first lieutenant. Nelson's contemporary 20 December 1796 dispatch names Lieutenant Culverhouse; British Library Nelson Papers Add MS 34905 f.325 names John Culverhouse and dates his letter to 1797. The malformed surname Culhouse can be corrected under the established source-grounded name-spelling policy. No bare surname alias is added. [Source](https://searcharchives.bl.uk/catalog/040-002087942) [Source](https://contemplator.com/history/NavalChronicleVol3-1799.pdf)
  Source: `src/derived/video-segments/2025-10-21_T18-30-06_nelson-a-very-british-god-of-war_1Qu6ezXIFgU.json`, segment `nelsons-own-patronage-network`.
- `louis-des-balbes-de-bertin-de-crillon` -> `louis-des-balbes-de-berton-de-crillon-duke-of-mahon`, display **Louis des Balbes de Berton de Crillon, Duke of Mahón**. The sole authored topic source follows Minorca's fall and the eighteenth-century commander's transfer to Gibraltar. The French Senate Grandjean archive index explicitly names Louis des Balbes de Berton de Crillon, duc de Mahon (1717-1796); the BnF manuscript catalog attributes the Gibraltar and Mahon siege papers to the same duke, and Institut Calvet identifies his 1717 birth and Minorca command. The French Culture Ministry separately identifies the sixteenth-century namesake (1541-1615). Correct Bertin to Berton and qualify this reviewed identity by Duke of Mahón. Neither the malformed unqualified source slug nor the corrected unqualified full name is a globally safe alias, because it could mean either man. [Source](https://archives.senat.fr/pdf/01_ArchivesEnLigne/Instruments_de_recherche/Instrument_de_recherche_Grandjean_final_pour_mise_en_ligne.pdf) [Source](https://ccfr.bnf.fr/portailccfr/ark%3A/16871/004D32D14152) [Source](https://www.institutcalvet.fr/en/node/30725) [Source](https://pop.culture.gouv.fr/notice/joconde/02860008927)
  Source: `src/derived/video-segments/2021-06-17_T17-30-23_patreon-26-the-great-seige-of-gibraltar-1779-1783_ZmELmZs2V4E.json`, segment `de-crillon-command-and-minorca`.

The Crillon spelling and identity are now resolved: Berton, Duke of Mahón, 1717-1796. Existing short-form Crillon prose is correct and stays unchanged. Both malformed and corrected unqualified full-name inputs receive review guards because a sixteenth-century namesake also exists.

These are systematic, bounded family and erroneous-name searches across the full authored shard set, not a claim that all 28,929 topic identities or all historical prose have received independent archival verification.

### Final name-repair and pruning checkpoint

Applied all 35 verified name substitutions: 23 public fields and 9 editorial evidence notes in six shards. Applied the Culverhouse and qualified Crillon topic corrections. The subsequent report found the retired unqualified Crillon record as its only unused topic and only review finding. Its exact authored reference count is zero; no manual description or aliases exist; both review guards are retained. Pruned that record, verified the registry schema, and byte-checked all 2,163 shards as unchanged by pruning.

An independent reconstruction from the original pre-task corpus verifies all 2,163 shards against the complete reviewed topic mappings and exact name edits. It found 137 changed shards and 248 changed topic arrays across the whole task, with every unrelated field preserved. At this checkpoint the final report is running; focused audits follow.
